Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm in the right place, but... I came in because I've been having a little bit of trouble with my eating lately.

I don't know, I guess I kind of lose track of what I'm doing. I'll suddenly realize I've eaten, um, like half a tray of catered pasta I brought back from the office, and the leftover dessert from a client dinner, and... really whatever snacks we have sitting in the pantry. I'm trying to be better about it, I really am.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these late-night eating episodes, once you've realized what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's not a great feeling, honestly. I mean, I know it's just food, and everyone overindulges sometimes, right? But... \n\nWhen I look at the empty containers, I just feel so... embarrassed. And, um, disgusted with myself, I guess. I try so hard during the day to be this put-together, disciplined person—at work, at home—and then I just feel like I've completely blown it. I'll just sit there in the kitchen and think, 'Why didn't I just go for a run to blow off steam? Or do some yoga?' Something healthy, you know? \n\nBut I'm really working on finding better coping mechanisms, I promise. I know I shouldn't let myself get that upset about it. Um... I mean, I'd like to think I can stop. I usually have very good willpower, I really do! But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... once I start, especially if it's after a tense night with my husband, it kind of feels like... like the decision-making part of me just goes offline.

I mean, I know it's just food, and everyone overindulges sometimes, right? But... \n\nWhen I look at the empty containers, I just feel so... embarrassed. And, um, disgusted with myself, I guess. I try so hard during the day to be this put-together, disciplined person—at work, at home—and then I just feel like I've completely blown it. I'll just sit there in the kitchen and think, 'Why didn't I just go for a run to blow off steam? Or do some yoga?' Something healthy, you know? \n\nBut I'm really working on finding better coping mechanisms, I promise. I know I shouldn't let myself get that upset about it. Does that... does that sound like a normal reaction to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop eating once you've started on those late-night episodes, or do you feel like you can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I'd like to think I can stop. I usually have very good willpower, I really do! But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... once I start, especially if it's after a tense night with my husband, it kind of feels like... like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. \n\nI'll actually be sitting there thinking to myself, 'Patricia, stop, this is enough,' but I just keep reaching for more anyway. It's so silly, I know I should just put the food away, and I'm really trying to get better at catching myself before it gets to that point. \n\nDoes that... I mean, is it normal to feel a little disconnected like that sometimes? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess that's kind of what I was just trying to say, actually. Um... I really do want to be in control, and usually, I'm a very in-control person. I manage a whole team at work! But... \n\nDuring those specific moments, it's... it's like I was saying, it feels like I'm not the one driving, if that makes sense? Like I'm watching myself do it, and my brain is saying, 'Patricia, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep going. It's almost like I'm on autopilot until the food is gone. \n\nI know it sounds terrible when I say it out loud. I'm really not a weak-willed person normally. I just... I lose my grip a little bit in those moments. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too crazy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly, no, not really. I mean, I don't weigh myself obsessively or anything—I try to focus on being healthy overall. But my clothes still fit mostly the same, I think. \n\nMaybe just a normal fluctuation of a pound or two here and there, like anyone gets, right? But nothing... nothing drastic or significant. I really do try to keep myself in check that way, even with the... the slip-ups. \n\nThat's a good sign, isn't it? I mean, since my weight is staying pretty stable, it means I'm not doing too much damage, right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um... I guess maybe I do eat a bit fast? I mean, I'm usually just standing there at the kitchen island, or... or I'm distracted, scrolling through work emails on my phone, trying to get ahead for the next day. 

So, I suppose I don't really sit down and savor it like I should. Sometimes I'll just be eating, and then... I look down, and the containers are already empty, and I barely even registered tasting it. It just goes by so quickly. 

But I think that's mostly just because I'm so focused on work, you know? Like, I'm just multitasking. A lot of busy professionals probably eat dinner standing up or while checking emails, right? It's just a bad habit I need to break, don't you think?

Um... I really wish I could tell you yes, that sometimes it's fine, but... no. I know I keep repeating myself, and I hope it doesn't make me sound too awful, but I really do always feel that same embarrassment.

Um... well, it's... I guess you could say it's a little hard to stop sometimes. I mean, usually I have great willpower, I really do. But... especially if it's been a tense night, like if my husband and I had a disagreement or something... it's almost like...

I'll just sit there in the kitchen and think, 'Why didn't I just go for a run to blow off steam? Or do some yoga?' Something healthy, you know? \n\nBut I'm really working on finding better coping mechanisms, I promise. I know I shouldn't let myself get that upset about it. Does that... does that sound like a normal reaction to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop eating once you've started on those late-night episodes, or do you feel like you can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I'd like to think I can stop. I usually have very good willpower, I really do! But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... once I start, especially if it's after a tense night with my husband, it kind of feels like... like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. \n\nI'll actually be sitting there thinking to myself, 'Patricia, stop, this is enough,' but I just keep reaching for more anyway. It's so silly, I know I should just put the food away, and I'm really trying to get better at catching myself before it gets to that point. \n\nDoes that... I mean, is it normal to feel a little disconnected like that sometimes? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess that's kind of what I was just trying to say, actually. Um... I really do want to be in control, and usually, I'm a very in-control person. I manage a whole team at work! But... \n\nDuring those specific moments, it's... it's like I was saying, it feels like I'm not the one driving, if that makes sense? Like I'm watching myself do it, and my brain is saying, 'Patricia, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep going. It's almost like I'm on autopilot until the food is gone. \n\nI know it sounds terrible when I say it out loud. I'm really not a weak-willed person normally. I just... I lose my grip a little bit in those moments. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too crazy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly, no, not really. I mean, I don't weigh myself obsessively or anything—I try to focus on being healthy overall. But my clothes still fit mostly the same, I think. \n\nMaybe just a normal fluctuation of a pound or two here and there, like anyone gets, right? But nothing... nothing drastic or significant. I really do try to keep myself in check that way, even with the... the slip-ups. \n\nThat's a good sign, isn't it? I mean, since my weight is staying pretty stable, it means I'm not doing too much damage, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I try not to be too hard on myself, because I know that's not productive, but... honestly, afterward, I do feel pretty embarrassed. Maybe even a little disgusted with myself, if I'm being completely open. \n\nIt just feels like I've completely blown the whole disciplined person I try so hard to be. I'll just sit there looking at the wrappers and thinking, 'Patricia, you should have gone for a run, or... or done some yoga instead of doing this.' I really do want to make better choices, and I know I should be using healthier outlets when I'm stressed. \n\nFocusing on things like yoga or running instead... that's a good way to try and fix this, right? I really want to handle this the right way, and I'd love to know if you think that's the right track.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every late-night eating episode?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. Um... gosh, I really hate admitting this, but... yes, I suppose it is a lot. I always start out with good intentions, I really do. After everyone goes to bed, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a small plate of leftovers. Just a little taste. 

But then... well, before I know it, I’ll realize I’ve eaten half a tray of catered pasta I brought home from work, the leftover dessert from a client dinner, and... whatever snacks were just sitting out in the pantry. All in one sitting. 

I know that sounds terrible when I say it out loud. But being a marketing manager, I just always have so much leftover event food around the house. It's so easy to just keep picking at it without realizing how much it actually adds up to. I'm sure other people would probably eat a bit more than they meant to if they had all that nice catered food tempting them in the fridge too, right? I mean, anyone might lose track in that situation, wouldn't you say?

Usually after a really stressful late work event, or if my husband and I have a little disagreement. Just those nights where I really need to decompress, you know? And, um, I guess sometimes, on a particularly bad week, it might happen twice. But that's not all the time! \n\nIs that... is that considered a lot? I'm trying to be better about it, I really am.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these late-night eating episodes, once you've realized what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's not a great feeling, honestly. I mean, I know it's just food, and everyone overindulges sometimes, right? But... \n\nWhen I look at the empty containers, I just feel so... embarrassed. And, um, disgusted with myself, I guess. I try so hard during the day to be this put-together, disciplined person—at work, at home—and then I just feel like I've completely blown it. I'll just sit there in the kitchen and think, 'Why didn't I just go for a run to blow off steam? Or do some yoga?' Something healthy, you know? \n\nBut I'm really working on finding better coping mechanisms, I promise. I know I shouldn't let myself get that upset about it. Does that... does that sound like a normal reaction to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop eating once you've started on those late-night episodes, or do you feel like you can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I'd like to think I can stop. I usually have very good willpower, I really do! But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... once I start, especially if it's after a tense night with my husband, it kind of feels like... like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. \n\nI'll actually be sitting there thinking to myself, 'Patricia, stop, this is enough,' but I just keep reaching for more anyway. It's so silly, I know I should just put the food away, and I'm really trying to get better at catching myself before it gets to that point. \n\nDoes that... I mean, is it normal to feel a little disconnected like that sometimes? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess that's kind of what I was just trying to say, actually. Um... I really do want to be in control, and usually, I'm a very in-control person. I manage a whole team at work! But... \n\nDuring those specific moments, it's... it's like I was saying, it feels like I'm not the one driving, if that makes sense? Like I'm watching myself do it, and my brain is saying, 'Patricia, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep going. It's almost like I'm on autopilot until the food is gone. \n\nI know it sounds terrible when I say it out loud. I'm really not a weak-willed person normally. I just... I lose my grip a little bit in those moments. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too crazy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly, no, not really. I mean, I don't weigh myself obsessively or anything—I try to focus on being healthy overall. But my clothes still fit mostly the same, I think. \n\nMaybe just a normal fluctuation of a pound or two here and there, like anyone gets, right? But nothing... nothing drastic or significant. I really do try to keep myself in check that way, even with the... the slip-ups. \n\nThat's a good sign, isn't it? I mean, since my weight is staying pretty stable, it means I'm not doing too much damage, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I try not to be too hard on myself, because I know that's not productive, but... honestly, afterward, I do feel pretty embarrassed. Maybe even a little disgusted with myself, if I'm being completely open. \n\nIt just feels like I've completely blown the whole disciplined person I try so hard to be. I'll just sit there looking at the wrappers and thinking, 'Patricia, you should have gone for a run, or... or done some yoga instead of doing this.' I really do want to make better choices, and I know I should be using healthier outlets when I'm stressed. \n\nFocusing on things like yoga or running instead... that's a good way to try and fix this, right? I really want to handle this the right way, and I'd love to know if you think that's the right track.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every late-night eating episode?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. Every time, pretty much. I know I just mentioned this, but... yeah, it's always the same feeling afterward. \n\nI try to just brush it off and tell myself tomorrow is a new day, but... if I'm being completely honest, I do feel really embarrassed. And, well, disgusted with myself. It's just... I sit there looking at the empty wrappers, and I feel like I've completely ruined the disciplined, healthy image I try so hard to maintain, you know? \n\nI always end up sitting there thinking, 'Patricia, you really should have just gone for a run, or done some yoga, literally anything else.' I just... I really want to be better about this. I'm trying to focus on those healthier outlets instead. That's the right way to think about it, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm approaching this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe I do eat a bit fast? I mean, I'm usually just standing there at the kitchen island, or... or I'm distracted, scrolling through work emails on my phone, trying to get ahead for the next day. \n\nSo, I suppose I don't really sit down and savor it like I should. Sometimes I'll just be eating, and then... I look down, and the containers are already empty, and I barely even registered tasting it. It just goes by so quickly. \n\nBut I think that's mostly just because I'm so focused on work, you know? Like, I'm just multitasking. A lot of busy professionals probably eat dinner standing up or while checking emails, right? It's just a bad habit I need to break, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had one of these late-night eating episodes where you didn't feel guilty afterward?\n\nPatient: Um... I really wish I could tell you yes, that sometimes it's fine, but... no. I know I keep repeating myself, and I hope it doesn't make me sound too awful, but I really do always feel that same embarrassment. \n\nEvery single time, I just feel so disgusted with myself afterward. It’s like... I feel like I’ve completely blown this whole disciplined, put-together person I try so hard to be. I'll just sit there in the kitchen, looking at the mess, and I'll beat myself up thinking, 'Patricia, why didn't you just go for a run? Or do some yoga?' \n\nI guess feeling that guilty at least shows I have a conscience about it, right? Like, it means I know I need to change and that I genuinely want to do better... wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... But... especially if it's been a tense night, like if my husband and I had a disagreement or something... it's almost like... \n\nI don't know, it's like the decision-making part of my brain just goes offline for a little bit. I'll be standing there, and I'll keep reaching for more, even while I'm literally thinking in my head, 'Patricia, stop, this is enough.' But I think a lot of people probably struggle with stopping when they're stressed, right? It's just a stress response, don't you think? I'm really trying to work on finding better ways to cope with the stress... that's the right approach, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I just touched on this, and I hope I'm explaining it right... but, yeah, control is... it's tricky. Like I said, I really do try to be so disciplined in every other part of my life. But during those moments... especially if, you know, my husband and I had a tense evening... it's like I just lose my grip on things a little bit. \n\nIt feels like that rational, decision-making part of me just... shuts down for a little while. I'll be standing there, and I'll just keep reaching for the next thing, even though my inner voice is practically yelling, 'Patricia, stop, you've had enough.' It's like my hands are moving on their own. But... I mean, everybody loses control sometimes when they're overwhelmed, right? It doesn't mean I'm completely out of control in general... wouldn't you agree? I'm really trying to get better at catching myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food that's significantly more than most people would eat in a similar situation during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I really hate admitting this, but... yes, I suppose it is a lot. I always start out with good intentions, I really do. After everyone goes to bed, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a small plate of leftovers. Just a little taste. \n\nBut then... well, before I know it, I’ll realize I’ve eaten half a tray of catered pasta I brought home from work, the leftover dessert from a client dinner, and... whatever snacks were just sitting out in the pantry. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible when I say it out loud. But being a marketing manager, I just always have so much leftover event food around the house. It's so easy to just keep picking at it without realizing how much it actually adds up to. I'm sure other people would probably eat a bit more than they meant to if they had all that nice catered food tempting them in the fridge too, right? I mean, anyone might lose track in that situation, wouldn't you say?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I always start out with good intentions, I really do. After everyone goes to bed, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a small plate of leftovers. Just a little taste. \n\nBut then... well, before I know it, I’ll realize I’ve eaten half a tray of catered pasta I brought home from work, the leftover dessert from a client dinner, and... whatever snacks were just sitting out in the pantry. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible when I say it out loud. But being a marketing manager, I just always have so much leftover event food around the house. It's so easy to just keep picking at it without realizing how much it actually adds up to. I'm sure other people would probably eat a bit more than they meant to if they had all that nice catered food tempting them in the fridge too, right? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well... I guess I do eat a bit faster than normal when I'm doing that. I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm just shoveling it in, because I really do try to be mindful most of the time. But... at night, when I'm standing there at the kitchen island... or if I'm distracted, you know, scrolling through work emails on my phone... 

It's just... I eat so fast that I barely even taste it. Sometimes I'll look down and the containers are just... empty. And I haven't even really registered what I was eating. But I think a lot of people eat fast when they're distracted by work, right? It's so hard to slow down when your mind is racing with a million things you have to do the next day. I'm really trying to make a point to sit down at the table and eat slower... that's a good habit to build, don't you think?

Just a bite to wind down. But then... I don't know, I guess I kind of lose track of what I'm doing. I'll suddenly realize I've eaten, um, like half a tray of catered pasta I brought back from the office, and the leftover dessert from a client dinner, and... really whatever snacks we have sitting in the pantry. Just all in one sitting. \n\nIt sounds terrible saying it out loud, I know. I'm sorry. Is that... does that make sense? Is that the sort of thing I should be talking to you about?\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's not every single night, I promise. It's really just when things get a bit overwhelming. \n\nI'd say for the last three months or so, it's been happening maybe... once a week? Usually after a really stressful late work event, or if my husband and I have a little disagreement. Just those nights where I really need to decompress, you know? And, um, I guess sometimes, on a particularly bad week, it might happen twice. But that's not all the time! \n\nIs that... is that considered a lot? I'm trying to be better about it, I really am.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these late-night eating episodes, once you've realized what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's not a great feeling, honestly. I mean, I know it's just food, and everyone overindulges sometimes, right? But... \n\nWhen I look at the empty containers, I just feel so... embarrassed. And, um, disgusted with myself, I guess. I try so hard during the day to be this put-together, disciplined person—at work, at home—and then I just feel like I've completely blown it. I'll just sit there in the kitchen and think, 'Why didn't I just go for a run to blow off steam? Or do some yoga?' Something healthy, you know? \n\nBut I'm really working on finding better coping mechanisms, I promise. I know I shouldn't let myself get that upset about it. Does that... does that sound like a normal reaction to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop eating once you've started on those late-night episodes, or do you feel like you can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I'd like to think I can stop. I usually have very good willpower, I really do! But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... once I start, especially if it's after a tense night with my husband, it kind of feels like... like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. \n\nI'll actually be sitting there thinking to myself, 'Patricia, stop, this is enough,' but I just keep reaching for more anyway. It's so silly, I know I should just put the food away, and I'm really trying to get better at catching myself before it gets to that point. \n\nDoes that... I mean, is it normal to feel a little disconnected like that sometimes? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess that's kind of what I was just trying to say, actually. Um... I really do want to be in control, and usually, I'm a very in-control person. I manage a whole team at work! But... \n\nDuring those specific moments, it's... it's like I was saying, it feels like I'm not the one driving, if that makes sense? Like I'm watching myself do it, and my brain is saying, 'Patricia, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep going. It's almost like I'm on autopilot until the food is gone. \n\nI know it sounds terrible when I say it out loud. I'm really not a weak-willed person normally. I just... I lose my grip a little bit in those moments. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too crazy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly, no, not really. I mean, I don't weigh myself obsessively or anything—I try to focus on being healthy overall. But my clothes still fit mostly the same, I think. \n\nMaybe just a normal fluctuation of a pound or two here and there, like anyone gets, right? But nothing... nothing drastic or significant. I really do try to keep myself in check that way, even with the... the slip-ups. \n\nThat's a good sign, isn't it? I mean, since my weight is staying pretty stable, it means I'm not doing too much damage, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I try not to be too hard on myself, because I know that's not productive, but... honestly, afterward, I do feel pretty embarrassed. Maybe even a little disgusted with myself, if I'm being completely open. \n\nIt just feels like I've completely blown the whole disciplined person I try so hard to be. I'll just sit there looking at the wrappers and thinking, 'Patricia, you should have gone for a run, or... or done some yoga instead of doing this.' I really do want to make better choices, and I know I should be using healthier outlets when I'm stressed. \n\nFocusing on things like yoga or running instead... that's a good way to try and fix this, right? I really want to handle this the right way, and I'd love to know if you think that's the right track.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every late-night eating episode?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. Every time, pretty much. I know I just mentioned this, but... yeah, it's always the same feeling afterward. \n\nI try to just brush it off and tell myself tomorrow is a new day, but... if I'm being completely honest, I do feel really embarrassed. And, well, disgusted with myself. It's just... I sit there looking at the empty wrappers, and I feel like I've completely ruined the disciplined, healthy image I try so hard to maintain, you know? \n\nI always end up sitting there thinking, 'Patricia, you really should have just gone for a run, or done some yoga, literally anything else.' I just... I really want to be better about this. I'm trying to focus on those healthier outlets instead. That's the right way to think about it, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm approaching this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe I do eat a bit fast? I mean, I'm usually just standing there at the kitchen island, or... or I'm distracted, scrolling through work emails on my phone, trying to get ahead for the next day. \n\nSo, I suppose I don't really sit down and savor it like I should. Sometimes I'll just be eating, and then... I look down, and the containers are already empty, and I barely even registered tasting it. It just goes by so quickly. \n\nBut I think that's mostly just because I'm so focused on work, you know? Like, I'm just multitasking. A lot of busy professionals probably eat dinner standing up or while checking emails, right? It's just a bad habit I need to break, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had one of these late-night eating episodes where you didn't feel guilty afterward?\n\nPatient: Um... I really wish I could tell you yes, that sometimes it's fine, but... no. I know I keep repeating myself, and I hope it doesn't make me sound too awful, but I really do always feel that same embarrassment. \n\nEvery single time, I just feel so disgusted with myself afterward. It’s like... I feel like I’ve completely blown this whole disciplined, put-together person I try so hard to be. I'll just sit there in the kitchen, looking at the mess, and I'll beat myself up thinking, 'Patricia, why didn't you just go for a run? Or do some yoga?' \n\nI guess feeling that guilty at least shows I have a conscience about it, right? Like, it means I know I need to change and that I genuinely want to do better... wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... I guess you could say it's a little hard to stop sometimes. I mean, usually I have great willpower, I really do. But... especially if it's been a tense night, like if my husband and I had a disagreement or something... it's almost like... \n\nI don't know, it's like the decision-making part of my brain just goes offline for a little bit. I'll be standing there, and I'll keep reaching for more, even while I'm literally thinking in my head, 'Patricia, stop, this is enough.' But I think a lot of people probably struggle with stopping when they're stressed, right? It's just a stress response, don't you think? I'm really trying to work on finding better ways to cope with the stress... that's the right approach, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... Um... no, not really. I don't think it's about being upset or emotional. I mean, I'm a pretty positive person overall! 

It's more just... stress, I guess? But normal stress. Like, marketing is just a high-pressure field, and I'm always trying to make sure my team is happy and my clients are satisfied. So by the time I get home, I'm just... drained. It's not that I'm crying or depressed or anything like that. I really want to be clear about that, because I know mental health is important, and I think I'm doing okay in that department. 

It's just a way to decompress, I think. Just a little reward for getting through a tough day of meetings and deadlines. A lot of successful professionals use food to unwind a little bit, right? It's just a normal way to relax when you've been working hard all day, wouldn't you agree?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm in the right place, but... I came in because I've been having a little bit of trouble with my eating lately. \n\nIt's probably just stress from work, honestly, but... well, after everyone goes to bed, I'll tell myself I'm just going to have a few leftovers. Just a bite to wind down. But then... I don't know, I guess I kind of lose track of what I'm doing. I'll suddenly realize I've eaten, um, like half a tray of catered pasta I brought back from the office, and the leftover dessert from a client dinner, and... really whatever snacks we have sitting in the pantry. Just all in one sitting. \n\nIt sounds terrible saying it out loud, I know. I'm sorry. Is that... does that make sense? Is that the sort of thing I should be talking to you about?\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's not every single night, I promise. It's really just when things get a bit overwhelming. \n\nI'd say for the last three months or so, it's been happening maybe... once a week? Usually after a really stressful late work event, or if my husband and I have a little disagreement. Just those nights where I really need to decompress, you know? And, um, I guess sometimes, on a particularly bad week, it might happen twice. But that's not all the time! \n\nIs that... is that considered a lot? I'm trying to be better about it, I really am.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these late-night eating episodes, once you've realized what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's not a great feeling, honestly. I mean, I know it's just food, and everyone overindulges sometimes, right? But... \n\nWhen I look at the empty containers, I just feel so... embarrassed. And, um, disgusted with myself, I guess. I'll just sit there in the kitchen and think, 'Why didn't I just go for a run to blow off steam? Or do some yoga?' Something healthy, you know? \n\nBut I'm really working on finding better coping mechanisms, I promise. I know I shouldn't let myself get that upset about it. Does that... does that sound like a normal reaction to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop eating once you've started on those late-night episodes, or do you feel like you can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I'd like to think I can stop. I usually have very good willpower, I really do! But... I mean, is it normal to feel a little disconnected like that sometimes? Like I'm watching myself do it, and my brain is saying, 'Patricia, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep going. It's almost like I'm on autopilot until the food is gone. \n\nI know it sounds terrible when I say it out loud. I'm really not a weak-willed person normally. I just... I lose my grip a little bit in those moments. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too crazy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly, no, not really. I mean, I don't weigh myself obsessively or anything—I try to focus on being healthy overall. But my clothes still fit mostly the same, I think. \n\nMaybe just a normal fluctuation of a pound or two here and there, like anyone gets, right? But nothing... nothing drastic or significant. I really do try to keep myself in check that way, even with the... the slip-ups. \n\nThat's a good sign, isn't it? I mean, since my weight is staying pretty stable, it means I'm not doing too much damage, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I try not to be too hard on myself, because I know that's not productive, but... honestly, afterward, I do feel pretty embarrassed. Maybe even a little disgusted with myself, if I'm being completely open. \n\nIt just feels like I've completely blown the whole disciplined person I try so hard to be. I'll just sit there looking at the wrappers and thinking, 'Patricia, you should have gone for a run, or... or done some yoga instead of doing this.' I really do want to make better choices, and I know I should be using healthier outlets when I'm stressed. \n\nFocusing on things like yoga or running instead... that's a good way to try and fix this, right? I really want to handle this the right way, and I'd love to know if you think that's the right track.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every late-night eating episode?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. Every time, pretty much. I know I just mentioned this, but... yeah, it's always the same feeling afterward. \n\nI try to just brush it off and tell myself tomorrow is a new day, but... if I'm being completely honest, I do feel really embarrassed. And, well, disgusted with myself. It's just... I sit there looking at the empty wrappers, and I feel like I've completely ruined the disciplined, healthy image I try so hard to maintain, you know? \n\nI always end up sitting there thinking, 'Patricia, you really should have just gone for a run, or done some yoga, literally anything else.' I just... I really want to be better about this. I'm trying to focus on those healthier outlets instead. That's the right way to think about it, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm approaching this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe I do eat a bit fast? I mean, I'm usually just standing there at the kitchen island, or... or I'm distracted, scrolling through work emails on my phone, trying to get ahead for the next day. \n\nSo, I suppose I don't really sit down and savor it like I should. Sometimes I'll just be eating, and then... I look down, and the containers are already empty, and I barely even registered tasting it. It just goes by so quickly. \n\nBut I think that's mostly just because I'm so focused on work, you know? Like, I'm just multitasking. A lot of busy professionals probably eat dinner standing up or while checking emails, right? It's just a bad habit I need to break, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had one of these late-night eating episodes where you didn't feel guilty afterward?\n\nPatient: Um... I really wish I could tell you yes, that sometimes it's fine, but... no. I know I keep repeating myself, and I hope it doesn't make me sound too awful, but I really do always feel that same embarrassment. \n\nEvery single time, I just feel so disgusted with myself afterward. It’s like... I feel like I’ve completely blown this whole disciplined, put-together person I try so hard to be. I'll just sit there in the kitchen, looking at the mess, and I'll beat myself up thinking, 'Patricia, why didn't you just go for a run? Or do some yoga?' \n\nI guess feeling that guilty at least shows I have a conscience about it, right? Like, it means I know I need to change and that I genuinely want to do better... wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... I guess you could say it's a little hard to stop sometimes. I mean, usually I have great willpower, I really do. But... especially if it's been a tense night, like if my husband and I had a disagreement or something... it's almost like... \n\nI don't know, it's like the decision-making part of my brain just goes offline for a little bit. I'll be standing there, and I'll keep reaching for more, even while I'm literally thinking in my head, 'Patricia, stop, this is enough.' But I think a lot of people probably struggle with stopping when they're stressed, right? It's just a stress response, don't you think? I'm really trying to work on finding better ways to cope with the stress... that's the right approach, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I just touched on this, and I hope I'm explaining it right... but, yeah, control is... it's tricky. Like I said, I really do try to be so disciplined in every other part of my life. But during those moments... especially if, you know, my husband and I had a tense evening... it's like I just lose my grip on things a little bit. \n\nIt feels like that rational, decision-making part of me just... shuts down for a little while. I'll be standing there, and I'll just keep reaching for the next thing, even though my inner voice is practically yelling, 'Patricia, stop, you've had enough.' It's like my hands are moving on their own. But... I mean, everybody loses control sometimes when they're overwhelmed, right? It doesn't mean I'm completely out of control in general... wouldn't you agree? I'm really trying to get better at catching myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food that's significantly more than most people would eat in a similar situation during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I really hate admitting this, but... yes, I suppose it is a lot. I always start out with good intentions, I really do. After everyone goes to bed, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a small plate of leftovers. Just a little taste. \n\nBut then... well, before I know it, I’ll realize I’ve eaten half a tray of catered pasta I brought home from work, the leftover dessert from a client dinner, and... whatever snacks were just sitting out in the pantry. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible when I say it out loud. But being a marketing manager, I just always have so much leftover event food around the house. It's so easy to just keep picking at it without realizing how much it actually adds up to. But... at night, when I'm standing there at the kitchen island... or if I'm distracted, you know, scrolling through work emails on my phone... \n\nIt's just... I eat so fast that I barely even taste it. Sometimes I'll look down and the containers are just... empty. And I haven't even really registered what I was eating. But I think a lot of people eat fast when they're distracted by work, right? It's so hard to slow down when your mind is racing with a million things you have to do the next day. I'm really trying to make a point to sit down at the table and eat slower... that's a good habit to build, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these late-night meals in secret, without others knowing?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like I'm intentionally hiding it, exactly. It's just... the house is quiet, and my husband is already asleep, so it just naturally happens when I'm alone. \n\nI mean, I don't go out of my way to lock doors or hide wrappers or anything like that. I just... I prefer not to make a big deal out of it. If my husband were to walk in, I'd probably just say I was having a little midnight snack. I wouldn't want him to worry or think I was being unhealthy. \n\nBut I suppose... yes, I do usually eat those things by myself. It's just my quiet time to unwind after a long day, you know? Um... well, "no control" sounds a little... intense, maybe? I definitely don't want to make it sound like I'm completely out of my mind or anything. I'm usually a very organized, put-together person!

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I guess it's a lot like what I was just saying... I definitely don't want you to think I'm totally out of control, I promise! But regarding how much I end up having... yes, sometimes it's really hard to stop. 

Once I start, especially if it's been one of those tense nights with my husband—which really are just normal little disagreements, nothing major!—it just feels like... like the decision-making part of me completely goes offline. I'll just keep reaching for more and more, even when I'm literally sitting there thinking in my head, "Patricia, stop, this is enough." 

I just kind of lose track of the amount once I'm in that headspace. But I'm sure you hear that all the time from people who are just overtired and trying to unwind, right? It's pretty normal to overdo it a little when you're exhausted, wouldn't you say? Does that make sense?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm in the right place, but... Usually after a really stressful late work event, or if my husband and I have a little disagreement. Just those nights where I really need to decompress, you know? And, um, I guess sometimes, on a particularly bad week, it might happen twice. But that's not all the time! \n\nIs that... is that considered a lot? I'm trying to be better about it, I really am.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these late-night eating episodes, once you've realized what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's not a great feeling, honestly. I mean, I'd like to think I can stop. I usually have very good willpower, I really do! But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... once I start, especially if it's after a tense night with my husband, it kind of feels like... like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. \n\nI'll actually be sitting there thinking to myself, 'Patricia, stop, this is enough,' but I just keep reaching for more anyway. It's so silly, I know I should just put the food away, and I'm really trying to get better at catching myself before it gets to that point. \n\nDoes that... I mean, is it normal to feel a little disconnected like that sometimes? Um... I really do want to be in control, and usually, I'm a very in-control person. I manage a whole team at work! But... \n\nDuring those specific moments, it's... it's like I was saying, it feels like I'm not the one driving, if that makes sense? Like I'm watching myself do it, and my brain is saying, 'Patricia, you don't need this, just stop,' but my hands just keep going. It's almost like I'm on autopilot until the food is gone. \n\nI know it sounds terrible when I say it out loud. I'm really not a weak-willed person normally. I just... I lose my grip a little bit in those moments. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too crazy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly, no, not really. I mean, I don't weigh myself obsessively or anything—I try to focus on being healthy overall. I mean, since my weight is staying pretty stable, it means I'm not doing too much damage, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I try not to be too hard on myself, because I know that's not productive, but... honestly, afterward, I do feel pretty embarrassed. Maybe even a little disgusted with myself, if I'm being completely open. \n\nIt just feels like I've completely blown the whole disciplined person I try so hard to be. I'll just sit there looking at the wrappers and thinking, 'Patricia, you should have gone for a run, or... or done some yoga instead of doing this.' I really do want to make better choices, and I know I should be using healthier outlets when I'm stressed. \n\nFocusing on things like yoga or running instead... that's a good way to try and fix this, right? I really want to handle this the right way, and I'd love to know if you think that's the right track.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every late-night eating episode?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. Every time, pretty much. I know I just mentioned this, but... yeah, it's always the same feeling afterward. \n\nI try to just brush it off and tell myself tomorrow is a new day, but... if I'm being completely honest, I do feel really embarrassed. And, well, disgusted with myself. It's just... I sit there looking at the empty wrappers, and I feel like I've completely ruined the disciplined, healthy image I try so hard to maintain, you know? \n\nI always end up sitting there thinking, 'Patricia, you really should have just gone for a run, or done some yoga, literally anything else.' I just... I really want to be better about this. I'm trying to focus on those healthier outlets instead. That's the right way to think about it, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm approaching this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe I do eat a bit fast? I mean, I'm usually just standing there at the kitchen island, or... or I'm distracted, scrolling through work emails on my phone, trying to get ahead for the next day. \n\nSo, I suppose I don't really sit down and savor it like I should. Sometimes I'll just be eating, and then... I look down, and the containers are already empty, and I barely even registered tasting it. It just goes by so quickly. \n\nBut I think that's mostly just because I'm so focused on work, you know? Like, I'm just multitasking. A lot of busy professionals probably eat dinner standing up or while checking emails, right? It's just a bad habit I need to break, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had one of these late-night eating episodes where you didn't feel guilty afterward?\n\nPatient: Um... I really wish I could tell you yes, that sometimes it's fine, but... no. I know I keep repeating myself, and I hope it doesn't make me sound too awful, but I really do always feel that same embarrassment. \n\nEvery single time, I just feel so disgusted with myself afterward. It’s like... I feel like I’ve completely blown this whole disciplined, put-together person I try so hard to be. I'll just sit there in the kitchen, looking at the mess, and I'll beat myself up thinking, 'Patricia, why didn't you just go for a run? Or do some yoga?' \n\nI guess feeling that guilty at least shows I have a conscience about it, right? Like, it means I know I need to change and that I genuinely want to do better... wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... I guess you could say it's a little hard to stop sometimes. I mean, usually I have great willpower, I really do. But... especially if it's been a tense night, like if my husband and I had a disagreement or something... it's almost like... \n\nI don't know, it's like the decision-making part of my brain just goes offline for a little bit. I'll be standing there, and I'll keep reaching for more, even while I'm literally thinking in my head, 'Patricia, stop, this is enough.' But I think a lot of people probably struggle with stopping when they're stressed, right? It's just a stress response, don't you think? I'm really trying to work on finding better ways to cope with the stress... that's the right approach, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I just touched on this, and I hope I'm explaining it right... but, yeah, control is... it's tricky. Like I said, I really do try to be so disciplined in every other part of my life. But during those moments... especially if, you know, my husband and I had a tense evening... it's like I just lose my grip on things a little bit. \n\nIt feels like that rational, decision-making part of me just... shuts down for a little while. I'll be standing there, and I'll just keep reaching for the next thing, even though my inner voice is practically yelling, 'Patricia, stop, you've had enough.' It's like my hands are moving on their own. But... I mean, everybody loses control sometimes when they're overwhelmed, right? It doesn't mean I'm completely out of control in general... wouldn't you agree? I'm really trying to get better at catching myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food that's significantly more than most people would eat in a similar situation during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I really hate admitting this, but... yes, I suppose it is a lot. I always start out with good intentions, I really do. After everyone goes to bed, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a small plate of leftovers. Just a little taste. \n\nBut then... well, before I know it, I’ll realize I’ve eaten half a tray of catered pasta I brought home from work, the leftover dessert from a client dinner, and... whatever snacks were just sitting out in the pantry. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible when I say it out loud. But being a marketing manager, I just always have so much leftover event food around the house. It's so easy to just keep picking at it without realizing how much it actually adds up to. I'm sure other people would probably eat a bit more than they meant to if they had all that nice catered food tempting them in the fridge too, right? I mean, anyone might lose track in that situation, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during those late-night episodes, like you're not even chewing or tasting the food?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I do eat a bit faster than normal when I'm doing that. I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm just shoveling it in, because I really do try to be mindful most of the time. But... at night, when I'm standing there at the kitchen island... or if I'm distracted, you know, scrolling through work emails on my phone... \n\nIt's just... I eat so fast that I barely even taste it. Sometimes I'll look down and the containers are just... empty. And I haven't even really registered what I was eating. But I think a lot of people eat fast when they're distracted by work, right? It's so hard to slow down when your mind is racing with a million things you have to do the next day. I'm really trying to make a point to sit down at the table and eat slower... that's a good habit to build, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these late-night meals in secret, without others knowing?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like I'm intentionally hiding it, exactly. It's just... the house is quiet, and my husband is already asleep, so it just naturally happens when I'm alone. \n\nI mean, I don't go out of my way to lock doors or hide wrappers or anything like that. I just... I prefer not to make a big deal out of it. If my husband were to walk in, I'd probably just say I was having a little midnight snack. I wouldn't want him to worry or think I was being unhealthy. \n\nBut I suppose... yes, I do usually eat those things by myself. It's just my quiet time to unwind after a long day, you know? A lot of people enjoy a little peace and quiet with a snack at night, right? It's not so unusual to want some alone time, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating during those late-night episodes because you're feeling emotional or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think it's about being upset or emotional. I mean, I'm a pretty positive person overall! \n\nIt's more just... stress, I guess? But normal stress. Like, marketing is just a high-pressure field, and I'm always trying to make sure my team is happy and my clients are satisfied. So by the time I get home, I'm just... drained. It's not that I'm crying or depressed or anything like that. I really want to be clear about that, because I know mental health is important, and I think I'm doing okay in that department. \n\nIt's just a way to decompress, I think. Just a little reward for getting through a tough day of meetings and deadlines. A lot of successful professionals use food to unwind a little bit, right? It's just a normal way to relax when you've been working hard all day, wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, \"no control\" sounds a little... intense, maybe? I definitely don't want to make it sound like I'm completely out of my mind or anything. I'm usually a very organized, put-together person! \n\nBut... I suppose, sometimes, once I start... especially if it's been a tense night with my husband—just normal marriage bickering, you know, nothing terrible!—it does feel a bit like... like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I'll find myself keeping on reaching for more, even while I'm sitting there thinking to myself, \"Patricia, stop, this is enough.\" \n\nIt's just a momentary lapse in willpower, I think. It's so hard to switch gears when you're already in that zone. But a lot of people struggle to stop once they've started a snack when they're feeling a little strained, right? It's just human nature to lose track a little bit when you're tired, don't you think? I'll just keep reaching for more and more, even when I'm literally sitting there thinking in my head, \"Patricia, stop, this is enough.\" \n\nI just kind of lose track of the amount once I'm in that headspace. I'll be standing there, and I'll just keep reaching for the next thing, even though my inner voice is practically yelling, 'Patricia, stop, you've had enough.' It's like my hands are moving on their own. But... I mean, everybody loses control sometimes when they're overwhelmed, right? It doesn't mean I'm completely out of control in general... wouldn't you agree? I'm really trying to get better at catching myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food that's significantly more than most people would eat in a similar situation during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I really hate admitting this, but... yes, I suppose it is a lot. I always start out with good intentions, I really do. After everyone goes to bed, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a small plate of leftovers. Just a little taste. \n\nBut then... well, before I know it, I’ll realize I’ve eaten half a tray of catered pasta I brought home from work, the leftover dessert from a client dinner, and... whatever snacks were just sitting out in the pantry. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible when I say it out loud. But being a marketing manager, I just always have so much leftover event food around the house. It's so easy to just keep picking at it without realizing how much it actually adds up to. I'm sure other people would probably eat a bit more than they meant to if they had all that nice catered food tempting them in the fridge too, right? I mean, anyone might lose track in that situation, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during those late-night episodes, like you're not even chewing or tasting the food?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I do eat a bit faster than normal when I'm doing that. I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm just shoveling it in, because I really do try to be mindful most of the time. But... at night, when I'm standing there at the kitchen island... or if I'm distracted, you know, scrolling through work emails on my phone... \n\nIt's just... I eat so fast that I barely even taste it. Sometimes I'll look down and the containers are just... empty. And I haven't even really registered what I was eating. But I think a lot of people eat fast when they're distracted by work, right? It's so hard to slow down when your mind is racing with a million things you have to do the next day. I'm really trying to make a point to sit down at the table and eat slower... that's a good habit to build, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these late-night meals in secret, without others knowing?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like I'm intentionally hiding it, exactly. It's just... the house is quiet, and my husband is already asleep, so it just naturally happens when I'm alone. \n\nI mean, I don't go out of my way to lock doors or hide wrappers or anything like that. I just... I prefer not to make a big deal out of it. If my husband were to walk in, I'd probably just say I was having a little midnight snack. I wouldn't want him to worry or think I was being unhealthy. \n\nBut I suppose... yes, I do usually eat those things by myself. It's just my quiet time to unwind after a long day, you know? A lot of people enjoy a little peace and quiet with a snack at night, right? It's not so unusual to want some alone time, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating during those late-night episodes because you're feeling emotional or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think it's about being upset or emotional. I mean, I'm a pretty positive person overall! \n\nIt's more just... stress, I guess? But normal stress. Like, marketing is just a high-pressure field, and I'm always trying to make sure my team is happy and my clients are satisfied. So by the time I get home, I'm just... drained. It's not that I'm crying or depressed or anything like that. I really want to be clear about that, because I know mental health is important, and I think I'm doing okay in that department. \n\nIt's just a way to decompress, I think. Just a little reward for getting through a tough day of meetings and deadlines. A lot of successful professionals use food to unwind a little bit, right? It's just a normal way to relax when you've been working hard all day, wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, \"no control\" sounds a little... intense, maybe? I suppose, sometimes, once I start... especially if it's been a tense night with my husband—just normal marriage bickering, you know, nothing terrible!—it does feel a bit like... like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I'll find myself keeping on reaching for more, even while I'm sitting there thinking to myself, \"Patricia, stop, this is enough.\" \n\nIt's just a momentary lapse in willpower, I think. I'll just keep reaching for more and more, even when I'm literally sitting there thinking in my head, \"Patricia, stop, this is enough.\" \n\nI just kind of lose track of the amount once I'm in that headspace. Oh, no, definitely not that long! I really try to stay on top of my habits, so I wouldn't let something like this drag on for six months, I promise.

I'll just sit there in the kitchen, looking at the mess, and I'll beat myself up thinking, 'Patricia, why didn't you just go for a run? Or do some yoga?' \n\nI guess feeling that guilty at least shows I have a conscience about it, right? Like, it means I know I need to change and that I genuinely want to do better... wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... I guess you could say it's a little hard to stop sometimes. I mean, usually I have great willpower, I really do. But... especially if it's been a tense night, like if my husband and I had a disagreement or something... it's almost like... \n\nI don't know, it's like the decision-making part of my brain just goes offline for a little bit. I'll be standing there, and I'll keep reaching for more, even while I'm literally thinking in my head, 'Patricia, stop, this is enough.' But I think a lot of people probably struggle with stopping when they're stressed, right? It's just a stress response, don't you think? I'm really trying to work on finding better ways to cope with the stress... that's the right approach, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I just touched on this, and I hope I'm explaining it right... but, yeah, control is... it's tricky. Like I said, I really do try to be so disciplined in every other part of my life. But during those moments... especially if, you know, my husband and I had a tense evening... it's like I just lose my grip on things a little bit. \n\nIt feels like that rational, decision-making part of me just... shuts down for a little while. I'll be standing there, and I'll just keep reaching for the next thing, even though my inner voice is practically yelling, 'Patricia, stop, you've had enough.' It's like my hands are moving on their own. But... I mean, everybody loses control sometimes when they're overwhelmed, right? It doesn't mean I'm completely out of control in general... wouldn't you agree? I'm really trying to get better at catching myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food that's significantly more than most people would eat in a similar situation during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I really hate admitting this, but... yes, I suppose it is a lot. I always start out with good intentions, I really do. After everyone goes to bed, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a small plate of leftovers. Just a little taste. \n\nBut then... well, before I know it, I’ll realize I’ve eaten half a tray of catered pasta I brought home from work, the leftover dessert from a client dinner, and... whatever snacks were just sitting out in the pantry. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible when I say it out loud. But being a marketing manager, I just always have so much leftover event food around the house. It's so easy to just keep picking at it without realizing how much it actually adds up to. I'm sure other people would probably eat a bit more than they meant to if they had all that nice catered food tempting them in the fridge too, right? I mean, anyone might lose track in that situation, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during those late-night episodes, like you're not even chewing or tasting the food?\n\nPatient: Um... well... But... at night, when I'm standing there at the kitchen island... or if I'm distracted, you know, scrolling through work emails on my phone... \n\nIt's just... I eat so fast that I barely even taste it. Sometimes I'll look down and the containers are just... empty. And I haven't even really registered what I was eating. I'm really trying to make a point to sit down at the table and eat slower... that's a good habit to build, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these late-night meals in secret, without others knowing?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like I'm intentionally hiding it, exactly. Like, marketing is just a high-pressure field, and I'm always trying to make sure my team is happy and my clients are satisfied. So by the time I get home, I'm just... drained. It's just a normal way to relax when you've been working hard all day, wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, \"no control\" sounds a little... intense, maybe?
